========================================
SUBJECT: LPM (120 questions)
========================================
Topics: {'Livestock Production Management', 'Livestock Production Management Core Diagnostic'}

001. [lpm_q_001] (Livestock Production Management) According to Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS) norms, what is the optimum covered floor space requirement for an adult dairy cow in a loose housing system?
     Ans: 3.5 square meters (approx 35-40 sq. ft.)
     Exp: Under BIS standards for loose housing, an adult cow requires 3.5 m2 of covered floor space and 7.0 m2 of open paddock ar...
002. [lpm_q_002] (Livestock Production Management) What is the BIS standard open paddock space requirement for an adult dairy buffalo in a loose housing system?
     Ans: 8.0 to 10.0 square meters
     Exp: Buffaloes require slightly more area than cows: 4.0 m2 covered floor space and 8.0 m2 open paddock area per adult animal...
003. [lpm_q_003] (Livestock Production Management) What is the recommended covered floor space for a breeding bull in a dedicated bull shed?
     Ans: 12.0 square meters
     Exp: Breeding bulls require an individual pen with at least 12.0 m2 covered area and 120.0 m2 open exercise paddock....
004. [lpm_q_004] (Livestock Production Management) The recommended floor space for a calving box (maternity pen) for a pregnant cow is:
     Ans: 10 to 12 square meters
     Exp: A maternity pen must provide 10-12 m2 of clean, well-bedded covered space to facilitate safe parturition and dam-calf bo...
005. [lpm_q_005] (Livestock Production Management) What is the recommended covered floor space requirement for an adult breeding ewe or doe (sheep/goat)?
     Ans: 1.0 to 1.5 square meters
     Exp: Adult sheep and goats require 1.0-1.5 m2 of covered pen area and 2.0-3.0 m2 of open run area....

SUBJECT: AGB (120 questions)

Topics: {'Animal Genetics & Breeding', 'Animal Genetics & Breeding Core Diagnostic'}

001. [agb_q_001] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) In a classic dihybrid cross (AaBb x AaBb) involving complete dominance and independent assortment, the expected phenotypic ratio in F2 progeny is:
     Ans: 9 : 3 : 3 : 1
     Exp: Under Mendel's second law (Independent Assortment), two unlinked heterozygous gene pairs segregate independently, produc...
002. [agb_q_002] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) A modified F2 phenotypic ratio of 9 : 7 is characteristic of which type of non-allelic gene interaction?
     Ans: Complementary gene action (duplicate recessive epistasis)
     Exp: Complementary gene interaction requires the presence of at least one dominant allele at both loci (A_B_) to express the ...
003. [agb_q_003] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) The classical phenotypic ratio of 12 : 3 : 1 in F2 generation is produced by which gene interaction?
     Ans: Dominant Epistasis
     Exp: Dominant epistasis occurs when a single dominant allele at the epistatic locus (e.g. A) masks the expression of the hypo...
004. [agb_q_004] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) The coat color inheritance in Labrador Retrievers (Black, Brown, Yellow) is a classic biological example of:
     Ans: Recessive Epistasis (9 : 3 : 4 ratio)
     Exp: The E/e locus is epistatic to B/b; homozygous recessive 'ee' prevents eumelanin deposition in the hair shaft, resulting ...
005. [agb_q_005] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) In poultry, comb shape inheritance involving Rose (R) and Pea (P) genes produces the new 'Walnut' comb in R_P_ and which single comb phenotype in rrpp?
     Ans: Single comb (rrpp)
     Exp: Bateson and Punnett demonstrated collaboration of two gene pairs producing Walnut (R_P_), Rose (R_pp), Pea (rrP_), and S...
006. [agb_q_006] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) The chromosomal sex-determination mechanism in domestic birds (chicken, turkey, duck) is:
     Ans: ZZ male and ZW female (female is heterogametic)
     Exp: In birds, the female is the heterogametic sex possessing ZW sex chromosomes, while the male is homogametic possessing ZZ...
007. [agb_q_007] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) The normal diploid (2n) chromosome number of the domestic river buffalo (Bubalus bubalis) is:
     Ans: 50 chromosomes
     Exp: River buffaloes (Murrah, Nili-Ravi) possess 2n = 50 chromosomes (5 pairs of submetacentric and 20 pairs of acrocentric a...
008. [agb_q_008] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) The normal diploid (2n) chromosome number of domestic cattle (Bos indicus / Bos taurus) is:
     Ans: 60 chromosomes
     Exp: Cattle possess 2n = 60 chromosomes; all 58 autosomes are strictly acrocentric, while the X and Y sex chromosomes are sub...

     Ans: 78 chromosomes

015. [agb_q_015] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) In a large random-mating population at Hardy-Weinberg equilibrium, if the frequency of a recessive allele (q) is 0.2, what is the expected frequency of heterozygous carriers (2pq)?
     Ans: 0.32 (32%)
     Exp: Since p + q = 1, p = 1 - 0.2 = 0.8. The heterozygous frequency is 2pq = 2 * 0.8 * 0.2 = 0.32 (32%)....
016. [agb_q_016] (Animal Genetics & Breeding) If the incidence of a homozygous recessive lethal disease (q^2) in a random mating herd is 1 in 10,000 (0.0001), what is the carrier (heterozygote) frequency?
     Ans: Approximately 0.02 (2% or 1 in 50 animals)
     Exp: q^2 = 0.0001, so q = 0.01. Thus, p = 1 - 0.01 = 0.99. Carrier frequency = 2pq = 2 * 0.99 * 0.01 = 0.0198 (~2% or 1 in 50...

========================================
PYQ SUBJECT: VPP (100 questions)
========================================
001. [pyq_vpp_001] The pathognomonic diagnostic microscopic lesion of Rabies is the presence of Negri bodies, which are located predominantly in:
     Ans: Pyramidal cells of hippocampus in carnivores and Purkinje cells of cerebellum in herbivores
002. [pyq_vpp_002] In Anthrax, which of the following is considered a pathognomonic triad on post-mortem examination of a bovine carcass?
     Ans: Failure of blood to clot (tarry black), absence of rigor mortis, and tremendous splenomegaly
003. [pyq_vpp_003] The classic 'button ulcers' (concentric necrotic ulcerative plaques) seen in the mucosa of the cecum and colon near the ileocecal orifice are pathognomonic for:
     Ans: Classical Swine Fever (Hog Cholera)
004. [pyq_vpp_004] The pathognomonic gross lesion described as 'turkey egg kidney' (subcapsular cortical petechial hemorrhages) is characteristic of:
     Ans: Classical Swine Fever (Hog Cholera)
005. [pyq_vpp_005] The 'honeycomb' or 'cerebriform' corrugation of the ileal and cecal mucosa that CANNOT be smoothed out by stretching is pathognomonic for:
     Ans: Johne’s Disease (Paratuberculosis / MAP)
006. [pyq_vpp_006] The 'tiger heart' (cor tigrinum / myocarditis with pale grayish-yellow stripes or patches) is seen in young calves dying acutely of:
     Ans: Foot and Mouth Disease (FMD)
007. [pyq_vpp_007] The 'rancid butter' (butyric acid) odor emitted upon incising dark, dry, spongy, crepitant muscle swellings is diagnostic of:
     Ans: Blackleg (Clostridium chauvoei)
008. [pyq_vpp_008] Under polarized light microscopy, amyloid deposits stained with Congo Red demonstrate which characteristic diagnostic optical property?
     Ans: Apple-green birefringence
009. [pyq_vpp_009] Zenker’s degeneration is a specific waxy / hyaline degeneration occurring classically in:
     Ans: Skeletal muscle fibers
010. [pyq_vpp_010] In tuberculosis, the multinucleated giant cells characterized by nuclei arranged in a horseshoe or peripheral wreath pattern are termed:
     Ans: Langhans giant cells
011. [pyq_vpp_011] Touton giant cells, which have a central ring of nuclei surrounded by a foamy lipid-rich cytoplasm, are characteristically seen in:
     Ans: Xanthomas and fat necrosis
012. [pyq_vpp_012] Reed-Sternberg cells (owl-eye binucleated giant cells) are diagnostic markers of:
     Ans: Hodgkin's lymphoma
013. [pyq_vpp_013] The 'nutmeg liver' gross appearance is produced by:
     Ans: Chronic passive venous congestion (congestive heart failure)
014. [pyq_vpp_014] The lines of Zahn are alternating pale and dark laminations found microscopically in:
     Ans: Ante-mortem thrombi
015. [pyq_vpp_015] The irreversible stage of cell injury characterized by nuclear shrinkage and increased basophilia (dark staining) is termed:
     Ans: Pyknosis
016. [pyq_vpp_016] Liquefactive necrosis is the characteristic response to ischemia in which anatomical structure?
     Ans: Brain and Spinal Cord (Central Nervous System)
017. [pyq_vpp_017] The 'bread-and-butter' heart is the classical gross description for:
     Ans: Fibrinous pericarditis (e.g. Traumatic Reticulopericarditis in cattle)
018. [pyq_vpp_018] The 'pipestem liver' appearance on necropsy of sheep or cattle is caused by chronic infection with:
     Ans: Fasciola hepatica / Fasciola gigantica
019. [pyq_vpp_019] The 'milk spot liver' in swine is caused by the larval migration of:
     Ans: Ascaris suum
020. [pyq_vpp_020] The 'pimply gut' characterized by hard caseocalcareous nodules scattered throughout the small and large intestinal walls of sheep is caused by:
     Ans: Oesophagostomum columbianum
021. [pyq_vpp_021] The 'hard pad disease' (marked hyperkeratosis of the footpads and nasal planum) in dogs is a classic manifestation of chronic:
     Ans: Canine Distemper Virus (CDV)
022. [pyq_vpp_022] Canine Distemper is histologically unique among viral diseases because it forms:
     Ans: Both intracytoplasmic AND intranuclear eosinophilic inclusion bodies
023. [pyq_vpp_023] Infectious Canine Hepatitis (Rubarth's disease / CAV-1) characteristically produces which type of inclusion bodies?
     Ans: Large basophilic intranuclear inclusion bodies (Cowdry Type A) in hepatocytes and vascular endothelium
024. [pyq_vpp_024] 'Blue eye' (transient or permanent corneal opacity / edema) in dogs convalescing from Infectious Canine Hepatitis is caused by:
     Ans: Type III hypersensitivity (immune-complex deposition in corneal endothelial cells)
025. [pyq_vpp_025] Diamond-shaped skin lesions (urticaria / diamond skin disease) in swine are pathognomonic for:
     Ans: Erysipelothrix rhusiopathiae (Swine Erysipelas)
026. [pyq_vpp_026] Vegetative valvular endocarditis with cauliflower-like friable thrombi on the mitral valve of pigs is most frequently caused by chronic:
     Ans: Erysipelothrix rhusiopathiae
027. [pyq_vpp_027] The pathognomonic lesion of 'Glanders' (Burkholderia mallei) in equids is the presence of nodules in the respiratory mucosa that ulcerate and heal leaving:
     Ans: Stellate (star-shaped) scars on the nasal septum
028. [pyq_vpp_028] Farcy in horses represents the cutaneous form of Glanders, characterized by:
     Ans: Nodules ('farcy buds') along subcutaneous lymphatics with thickening of vessels ('farcy pipes')
029. [pyq_vpp_029] The 'zebra striping' (longitudinal congested stripes on the mucosa of the rectum and colon) is pathognomonic for:
     Ans: Rinderpest (and Peste des Petits Ruminants - PPR)
030. [pyq_vpp_030] The primary tumor marker / diagnostic chromosome abnormality in Canine Transmissible Venereal Tumor (CTVT) is a diploid chromosome number of:
     Ans: 59 chromosomes (compared to the normal canine 78)
031. [pyq_vpp_031] Which neoplasm is derived from tissue of all three embryonic germ layers (ectoderm, mesoderm, and endoderm)?
     Ans: Teratoma
032. [pyq_vpp_032] A benign developmental mass composed of mature, disorganized tissues normal to that specific anatomical site is a:
     Ans: Hamartoma
033. [pyq_vpp_033] A mass of histologically normal tissue present in an abnormal anatomical location is termed a:
     Ans: Choristoma (heterotopia)
034. [pyq_vpp_034] The most common primary malignant bone tumor in large-breed dogs, presenting with a 'sunburst' periosteal reaction on radiographs, is:
     Ans: Osteosarcoma
035. [pyq_vpp_035] Canine mast cell tumors (MCT) contain cytoplasmic granules that exhibit metachromasia (staining purple-red with blue dyes like Toluidine Blue) because of:
     Ans: Heparin and sulfated glycosaminoglycans
036. [pyq_vpp_036] In avian pathology, 'Bollinger bodies' are diagnostic large intracytoplasmic inclusion bodies observed in epithelial cells in:
     Ans: Fowl Pox
037. [pyq_vpp_037] Infectious Laryngotracheitis (ILT) in domestic fowl produces which diagnostic inclusion bodies?
     Ans: Intranuclear inclusion bodies in tracheal epithelial cells
038. [pyq_vpp_038] The 'water-belly' condition in feedlot steers and wethers is the pathological sequel of:
     Ans: Rupture of the urinary bladder or urethra secondary to obstructive urolithiasis
039. [pyq_vpp_039] The 'pulpy kidney' lesion in sheep is pathognomonic for enterotoxemia caused by which clostridial organism?
     Ans: Clostridium perfringens Type D
040. [pyq_vpp_040] 'Black disease' (Infectious Necrotic Hepatitis) in sheep is triggered by migration of immature Fasciola flukes in liver tissue creating anaerobic niches for:
     Ans: Clostridium novyi Type B
041. [pyq_vpp_041] 'Braxy' in sheep is an acute abomasitis characterized by severe mucosal edema, necrosis, and gas production caused by:
     Ans: Clostridium septicum
042. [pyq_vpp_042] The classical pathological lesion in cattle with 'Bovine Spongiform Encephalopathy' (BSE) consists of:
     Ans: Bilateral symmetrical vacuolation (spongiform change) of neuronal perikarya and neuropil in the obex
043. [pyq_vpp_043] Microabscesses in the brainstem (medulla oblongata) with marked mononuclear perivascular cuffing in ruminants with circling signs are diagnostic of:
     Ans: Listeriosis (Listeria monocytogenes / Circling Disease)
044. [pyq_vpp_044] Autofluorescence of the cerebral cortex under long-wave ultraviolet light (Wood's lamp, 365 nm) is diagnostic of:
     Ans: Polioencephalomalacia (Cerebrocortical necrosis) due to thiamine deficiency or sulfur excess
045. [pyq_vpp_045] The primary lesion of 'Malignant Catarrhal Fever' (MCF) in cattle and bison is:
     Ans: Generalized lymphocytic vasculitis and perivascular cuffing with fibrinoid vascular necrosis
046. [pyq_vpp_046] In 'Enzootic Bovine Hematuria' (EBH), chronic ingestion of bracken fern (Pteridium aquilinum) containing ptaquiloside induces:
     Ans: Hemangiomas, hemangiosarcomas, and transitional cell carcinomas of the urinary bladder
047. [pyq_vpp_047] The gross lesion of 'Contagious Bovine Pleuropneumonia' (CBPP / Mycoplasma mycoides subsp. mycoides) is classically described as:
     Ans: 'Marbled lung' with massive widening of interlobular septa by serofibrinous exudate and sequestrum formation
048. [pyq_vpp_048] The presence of 'heart failure cells' in the pulmonary alveoli refers to:
     Ans: Alveolar macrophages laden with golden-brown hemosiderin pigment from phagocytosed extravasated red blood cells
049. [pyq_vpp_049] The pathological lesion termed 'proud flesh' in horses represents excessive exuberant proliferation of:
     Ans: Granulation tissue (fibroblasts and endothelial capillaries)
050. [pyq_vpp_050] A thrombus that forms in the caudal abdominal aorta at its bifurcation into external and internal iliac arteries in cats with hypertrophic cardiomyopathy is known as a:
     Ans: 'Saddle thrombus'
051. [pyq_vpp_051] The 'Aschoff bodies' are pathognomonic granulomatous myocardial nodules seen in:
     Ans: Rheumatic myocarditis
052. [pyq_vpp_052] The 'Anitschkow myocytes' (caterpillar cells) found in cardiac lesions are characterized by:
     Ans: An elongated wavy ribbon-like nucleus resembling a caterpillar
053. [pyq_vpp_053] The 'sago spleen' pattern of amyloidosis results from amyloid deposition selectively occurring in the:
     Ans: Splenic follicles (Malpighian corpuscles / white pulp)
054. [pyq_vpp_054] The 'lardaceous spleen' pattern of amyloidosis involves diffuse deposition in the:
     Ans: Walls of the red pulp venous sinusoids and splenic cords
055. [pyq_vpp_055] The 'splinter hemorrhage' or 'flame-shaped hemorrhage' in tissues is typically located in:
     Ans: Nerve fiber layer of the retina or along striated muscle fascicles
056. [pyq_vpp_056] The primary tumor suppressor gene frequently mutated or deleted in more than 50% of canine malignant tumors is:
     Ans: p53 gene
057. [pyq_vpp_057] Retinoblastoma gene (Rb) prevents excessive cell growth by inhibiting the cell cycle until a cell is ready to divide, acting at which checkpoint?
     Ans: G1 to S phase transition
058. [pyq_vpp_058] The primary microscopic criterion for grading the malignancy of mast cell tumors according to the Patnaik system is:
     Ans: Depth of invasion, cellular differentiation, and degree of cytoplasmic granulation
059. [pyq_vpp_059] The 'strawberry gallbladder' in dogs and humans is caused by mucosal accumulation of:
     Ans: Cholesterol inside macrophages (Cholesterolosis)
060. [pyq_vpp_060] The primary cause of 'dry gangrene' of the extremities (ears, tail, hooves) in cattle is:
     Ans: Ergotism (ingestion of Claviceps purpurea sclerotia) or Fescue foot
061. [pyq_vpp_061] The microscopic lesion characterized by 'onion-skin' concentric lamination in splenic arterioles is classic for:
     Ans: Systemic Lupus Erythematosus (SLE)
062. [pyq_vpp_062] In 'Canine Parvovirus Enteritis', the primary cellular target of viral destruction is the:
     Ans: Actively dividing epithelial cells in the crypts of Lieberkühn and lymphoid tissues
063. [pyq_vpp_063] In contrast to Parvovirus, 'Rotavirus' and 'Coronavirus' in calves and piglets target and destroy:
     Ans: Mature absorptive enterocytes on the upper two-thirds of the intestinal villi (sparing crypts)
064. [pyq_vpp_064] The 'blue-tongue' appearance (cyanosis of the tongue) in sheep with Bluetongue virus is caused by:
     Ans: Orbivirus-induced endothelial cell necrosis resulting in microthrombi and severe venous stasis
065. [pyq_vpp_065] The 'button-like' or 'volcano-like' crateriform mucosal ulcers on the tongue and dental pad in Bovine Viral Diarrhea (Mucosal Disease) represent:
     Ans: Acute erosive and ulcerative stomatitis with mucosal epithelial ballooning necrosis
066. [pyq_vpp_066] The 'lead line' on the gingival margins in animals with chronic lead poisoning is caused by the precipitation of:
     Ans: Lead sulfide
067. [pyq_vpp_067] The 'Satellitosis' and 'Neuronophagia' observed in viral encephalitis refer to:
     Ans: Accumulation of oligodendrocytes/microglia around degenerating neurons followed by their phagocytosis
068. [pyq_vpp_068] The microscopic lesion known as 'fibrinoid necrosis' typically occurs in:
     Ans: Blood vessel walls affected by immune-complex vasculitis (Type III) or malignant hypertension
069. [pyq_vpp_069] The primary chemical mediator of acute vascular permeability responsible for the immediate transient phase (first 15-30 minutes) is:
     Ans: Histamine and Serotonin
070. [pyq_vpp_070] The cytokine primarily responsible for inducing fever (pyrogenesis) by acting on the hypothalamic thermoregulatory center is:
     Ans: Interleukin-1 (IL-1) and Tumor Necrosis Factor-alpha (TNF-alpha)
071. [pyq_vpp_071] The principal chemokine responsible for directed chemotaxis of neutrophils toward bacterial infection sites is:
     Ans: Interleukin-8 (CXCL8)
072. [pyq_vpp_072] The 'opsonin' that most powerfully enhances macrophage phagocytosis of bacteria is:
     Ans: Fc portion of IgG antibodies and complement fragment C3b
073. [pyq_vpp_073] The 'respiratory burst' in activated neutrophils that generates microbicidal reactive oxygen species (superoxide anion) is catalyzed by:
     Ans: NADPH oxidase (phagocyte oxidase)
074. [pyq_vpp_074] Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) in dogs is an inherited genetic defect characterized by the deficiency of:
     Ans: NADPH oxidase, preventing the generation of microbicidal reactive oxygen radicals
075. [pyq_vpp_075] Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (BLAD in Holstein cattle and CLAD in Irish Setters) is caused by a mutation in:
     Ans: CD18 (Integrin beta-2 subunit), preventing neutrophil firm adhesion and extravasation
076. [pyq_vpp_076] The hallmark microscopic lesion of 'Canine Infectious Tracheobronchitis' (Kennel Cough) is:
     Ans: Loss of ciliated tracheal epithelium with purulent or mucopurulent exudate and Bordetella adherence
077. [pyq_vpp_077] The 'hemosiderosis' seen in the spleen and liver of animals with autoimmune hemolytic anemia (AIHA) results from:
     Ans: Massive extravascular erythrophagocytosis and breakdown of hemoglobin by macrophages
078. [pyq_vpp_078] The microscopic appearance of 'Gout' in birds and reptiles consists of periarticular and visceral deposits of:
     Ans: Needle-shaped radiating monosodium urate crystals surrounded by foreign-body giant cells (tophi)
079. [pyq_vpp_079] The pathognomonic renal lesion described as 'white spotted kidney' in young calves is caused by focal interstitial nephritis induced by:
     Ans: Escherichia coli bacteremia
080. [pyq_vpp_080] Pulmonary 'emphysema' is anatomically defined as:
     Ans: Permanent abnormal enlargement of air spaces distal to terminal bronchioles accompanied by destruction of alveolar walls
081. [pyq_vpp_081] The 'bloat line' observed in the esophagus on necropsy of cattle dying from acute ruminal tympany is characterized by:
     Ans: Sharp line of demarcation between the pale, ischemic cervical esophagus and congested thoracic/abdominal esophagus
082. [pyq_vpp_082] The characteristic microscopic brain lesion of 'Scrapie' in sheep is:
     Ans: Neuronal vacuolation (intracytoplasmic clear vacuoles) in the dorsal motor nucleus of the vagus and reticular formation
083. [pyq_vpp_083] The 'sawhorse' stance in horses and cattle with Tetanus is a manifestation of:
     Ans: Generalized persistent tonic contraction of extensor skeletal muscles due to disinhibition of motor neurons
084. [pyq_vpp_084] The diagnostic lesion termed 'hyaline membranes' in acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS / Shock Lung) is composed of:
     Ans: Fibrin, cellular debris, and surfactant remnants lining the alveolar walls
085. [pyq_vpp_085] The 'cuffing' of blood vessels by lymphocytes and plasma cells in the brain (perivascular cuffing) is a classic histological hallmark of:
     Ans: Non-suppurative viral encephalitis
086. [pyq_vpp_086] The 'syncytia' (multinucleated giant epithelial cells) observed in bovine lungs in Bovine Respiratory Syncytial Virus (BRSV) infection are produced by the action of:
     Ans: Viral Fusion (F) protein causing adjacent host cell membranes to coalesce
087. [pyq_vpp_087] The pathognomonic lesion of 'Canine Parvovirus Myocarditis' in young pups infected in utero or before 4 weeks of age is:
     Ans: Non-suppurative necrotizing myocarditis with basophilic intranuclear inclusion bodies in cardiomyocytes
088. [pyq_vpp_088] The 'parrot mouth' (brachygnathia) in cattle and horses refers to:
     Ans: Shortening of the mandible relative to the maxilla
089. [pyq_vpp_089] The 'bull-dog calf' anomaly in Dexter cattle is a lethal genetic chondrodysplasia characterized by:
     Ans: Extreme micromelia, compressed vertebral column, and protruding tongue due to aggrecan gene mutation
090. [pyq_vpp_090] The 'parakeratosis' in pigs is histopathologically recognized by:
     Ans: Persistence of pyknotic nuclei in the stratum corneum with thickening of the epidermis
091. [pyq_vpp_091] The 'hyperkeratosis' seen in cattle with chronic Chlorinated Naphthalene poisoning (X-disease) is caused by interference with:
     Ans: Vitamin A metabolism
092. [pyq_vpp_092] The characteristic lesion of 'Black quarter' (Clostridium chauvoei) in cattle occurs primarily in which muscles?
     Ans: Large heavy skeletal muscles (thigh, shoulder, rump, brisket)
093. [pyq_vpp_093] The term 'sequestrum' in bone pathology refers to:
     Ans: A detached piece of dead necrotic bone surrounded by ischemic exudate
094. [pyq_vpp_094] The primary cause of 'osteodystrophia fibrosa' (big head disease / rubber jaw) in horses and goats is:
     Ans: Secondary Hyperparathyroidism (nutritional Ca:P imbalance or chronic renal failure)
095. [pyq_vpp_095] 'Rickets' in growing young animals is characterized histologically by:
     Ans: Failure of osteoid mineralization and persistence of hypertrophic chondrocyte columns at the growth plate (physis)
096. [pyq_vpp_096] In adult animals, the counterpart of rickets occurring AFTER growth plates have closed is:
     Ans: Osteomalacia
097. [pyq_vpp_097] 'Osteoporosis' is distinct from osteomalacia because osteoporosis represents:
     Ans: A reduction in total bone mass where the bone that remains is normally mineralized
098. [pyq_vpp_098] The 'goiter' (non-neoplastic, non-inflammatory enlargement of the thyroid gland) is histologically characterized by:
     Ans: Follicular epithelial hyperplasia and hypertrophy in response to prolonged elevated TSH
099. [pyq_vpp_099] The 'cataract' is clinically and pathologically defined as:
     Ans: Any opacity or clouding of the crystalline lens of the eye or its capsule
100. [pyq_vpp_100] The primary lesion in 'Canine Glaucoma' that causes retinal ganglion cell death and blindness is:
     Ans: Sustained elevation of intraocular pressure (IOP) due to impaired aqueous humor outflow at the iridocorneal angle

========================================
PYQ SUBJECT: VMC (100 questions)
========================================
001. [pyq_vmc_001] The 'McFadyean reaction' used for presumptive diagnosis of Anthrax in blood smears stains which component of Bacillus anthracis?
     Ans: Capsule (poly-D-glutamic acid) pink/purple with blue bacterial cell body using polychrome methylene blue
002. [pyq_vmc_002] The unique capsule of Bacillus anthracis is chemically composed of:
     Ans: Poly-D-glutamic acid (polypeptide)
003. [pyq_vmc_003] The lethal toxin of Bacillus anthracis is composed of which two synergistic proteins?
     Ans: Protective Antigen (PA) + Lethal Factor (LF)
004. [pyq_vmc_004] The 'Ascoli thermoprecipitin test' is a diagnostic ring precipitation test used to detect anthrax antigens in:
     Ans: Hides, skins, and decomposing tissues of suspected carcasses
005. [pyq_vmc_005] The 'Medusa head' colony appearance on nutrient agar is characteristic of:
     Ans: Bacillus anthracis
006. [pyq_vmc_006] The 'stormy fermentation' of litmus milk (rapid acid, gas production, and clot tearing) is pathognomonic for:
     Ans: Clostridium perfringens
007. [pyq_vmc_007] The 'drumstick' or 'tennis racket' morphology of bacterial cells with terminal, spherical, bulging spores is characteristic of:
     Ans: Clostridium tetani
008. [pyq_vmc_008] Tetanospasmin, the potent neurotoxin produced by Clostridium tetani, acts by:
     Ans: Preventing the release of inhibitory neurotransmitters (GABA and Glycine) at Renshaw cells
009. [pyq_vmc_009] Botulinum neurotoxin causes flaccid paralysis by selectively blocking the release of which neurotransmitter?
     Ans: Acetylcholine at the peripheral neuromuscular junction
010. [pyq_vmc_010] The 'Nagler reaction' used for rapid presumptive identification of Clostridium perfringens demonstrates:
     Ans: Alpha-toxin lecithinase (phospholipase C) activity inhibited by specific antitoxin on egg yolk agar
011. [pyq_vmc_011] The 'pulpy kidney disease' (enterotoxemia) in rapidly growing, well-nourished sheep and lambs is caused by:
     Ans: Clostridium perfringens Type D (Epsilon toxin)
012. [pyq_vmc_012] The distinctive 'umbrella-shaped' or 'tumbling motility' seen in semi-solid agar at 22-25°C but NOT at 37°C is diagnostic of:
     Ans: Listeria monocytogenes
013. [pyq_vmc_013] The 'CAMP test' (Christie, Atkins, Munch-Petersen) shows an arrowhead-shaped synergistic zone of beta-hemolysis between:
     Ans: Streptococcus agalactiae and beta-lysin producing Staphylococcus aureus
014. [pyq_vmc_014] The 'test-tube brush' or 'lamp-brush' growth pattern in gelatin stab cultures incubated at 22°C is typical of:
     Ans: Erysipelothrix rhusiopathiae
015. [pyq_vmc_015] In cattle, pyelonephritis characterized by ascending hemorrhagic purulent cystitis and ureteritis is primarily caused by:
     Ans: Corynebacterium renale
016. [pyq_vmc_016] The etiologic agent of 'Caseous Lymphadenitis' (CLA / pseudotuberculosis / onion-ring lymph nodes) in sheep and goats is:
     Ans: Corynebacterium pseudotuberculosis
017. [pyq_vmc_017] The 'wooden tongue' (actinobacillosis) in cattle with granulomatous induration of the tongue is caused by which Gram-negative bacterium?
     Ans: Actinobacillus lignieresii
018. [pyq_vmc_018] The 'lumpy jaw' in cattle is a rarefying osteomyelitis of the mandible caused by:
     Ans: Actinomyces bovis
019. [pyq_vmc_019] The 'sulfur granules' discharged from sinus tracts in bovine lumpy jaw consist microscopically of:
     Ans: Colonies of Actinomyces bovis surrounded by radiating, club-like Splendore-Hoeppli proteinaceous precipitates
020. [pyq_vmc_020] Which selective and differential medium containing tellurite is routinely used for isolation of Corynebacterium species?
     Ans: Hoyle’s tellurite agar / Tinsdale medium
021. [pyq_vmc_021] Which serological typing scheme is globally utilized for classifying Salmonella based on somatic (O) and flagellar (H) antigens?
     Ans: Kauffmann-White-Le Minor scheme
022. [pyq_vmc_022] The causative agent of 'Pullorum disease' (bacillary white diarrhea) in neonatal chicks is:
     Ans: Salmonella enterica subsp. enterica serovar Gallinarum-Pullorum (non-motile)
023. [pyq_vmc_023] Fowl Typhoid in adult poultry is caused by:
     Ans: Salmonella Gallinarum
024. [pyq_vmc_024] The etiological agent of Fowl Cholera in chickens and turkeys is:
     Ans: Pasteurella multocida
025. [pyq_vmc_025] The specific capsular serotypes of Pasteurella multocida responsible for Hemorrhagic Septicemia (HS) in cattle and buffaloes in Asia and Africa are:
     Ans: B:2 (Asian serotype) and E:2 (African serotype) (Carter and Heddleston classification)
026. [pyq_vmc_026] The diagnostic 'bipolar staining' (safety-pin appearance) seen with Giemsa or Leishman stain in blood smears is typical of:
     Ans: Pasteurella multocida and Yersinia pestis
027. [pyq_vmc_027] The causative agent of 'Infectious Coryza' in poultry, characterized by acute seromucoid nasal discharge, facial edema, and conjunctivitis, is:
     Ans: Avibacterium paragallinarum (requires V-factor / NAD)
028. [pyq_vmc_028] The satellite phenomenon around Staphylococcus aureus streak on blood agar occurs because S. aureus provides:
     Ans: V factor (NAD / Nicotinamide Adenine Dinucleotide)
029. [pyq_vmc_029] The 'Shiga-toxin producing Escherichia coli' (STEC / EHEC) serotype classically associated with Hemolytic Uremic Syndrome (HUS) and hemorrhagic enteritis is:
     Ans: E. coli O157:H7
030. [pyq_vmc_030] The principal fimbrial (pilus) adhesin in Enterotoxigenic E. coli (ETEC) causing neonatal calf scours is:
     Ans: K99 (F5)
031. [pyq_vmc_031] The 'Rose Bengal Plate Test' (RBPT) used as a rapid screening herd test for Brucellosis utilizes an antigen buffered at which pH?
     Ans: Acidic pH (3.65)
032. [pyq_vmc_032] The 'Milk Ring Test' (MRT) for herd screening of bovine brucellosis detects Brucella antibodies in milk through agglutination of hematoxylin-stained antigen with which milk component?
     Ans: Fat globules (cream layer) coated with agglutinating IgA/IgM antibodies
033. [pyq_vmc_033] The 'Standard Tube Agglutination Test' (STAT) for bovine brucellosis is expressed in International Units, and a diagnostic titer indicative of infection in non-vaccinated cattle is typically:
     Ans: >= 100 IU/ml (or 1:50 to 1:100 dilution)
034. [pyq_vmc_034] Brucella abortus has an intense tissue tropism for the gravid ruminant placenta and fetal fluids primarily because of high concentrations of which growth-stimulatory polyol?
     Ans: Erythritol
035. [pyq_vmc_035] Which staining procedure is used to identify Mycobacterium tuberculosis and Mycobacterium bovis based on their cell wall mycolic acid content?
     Ans: Ziehl-Neelsen (Acid-Fast) stain
036. [pyq_vmc_036] The 'Single Intradermal Comparative Cervical Tuberculin' (SICCT) test in cattle differentiates M. bovis infection from sensitization to environmental mycobacteria by comparing skin swelling to:
     Ans: Bovine PPD and Avian PPD injected intradermally at two sites on the neck
037. [pyq_vmc_037] Mycobacterium avium subsp. paratuberculosis (MAP), the causative agent of Johne's disease, strictly requires which iron-chelating growth factor in culture media?
     Ans: Mycobactin (e.g. Mycobactin J)
038. [pyq_vmc_038] The 'silver impregnation technique' (Fontana stain or Levaditi stain) is standardly used for demonstrating:
     Ans: Spirochaetes (Leptospira and Treponema)
039. [pyq_vmc_039] The 'Microscopic Agglutination Test' (MAT) is the definitive gold standard serological test for the diagnosis of:
     Ans: Leptospirosis
040. [pyq_vmc_040] The causative agent of 'Swine Dysentery', characterized by mucohemorrhagic colitis and typhlitis in feeder pigs, is:
     Ans: Brachyspira hyodysenteriae
041. [pyq_vmc_041] The 'Straus reaction' (acute purulent periorchitis and testicular enlargement in inoculated male guinea pigs) is classically produced by:
     Ans: Burkholderia mallei and Brucella ovis
042. [pyq_vmc_042] The diagnostic 'Mallein test' for diagnosing latent and chronic Glanders in equines is routinely performed via which route?
     Ans: Intrapalpebral (injection into lower eyelid dermis) or eye drop ophthalmic route
043. [pyq_vmc_043] The 'fried egg' colony appearance on specialized agar (PPLO agar supplemented with serum and yeast extract) is characteristic of:
     Ans: Mycoplasma species
044. [pyq_vmc_044] The causative agent of 'Contagious Bovine Pleuropneumonia' (CBPP), producing marbling of lungs and sequestrum formation, is:
     Ans: Mycoplasma mycoides subsp. mycoides (small colony type)
045. [pyq_vmc_045] The causative agent of 'Contagious Caprine Pleuropneumonia' (CCPP) in goats is:
     Ans: Mycoplasma capricolum subsp. capripneumoniae
046. [pyq_vmc_046] The 'blue-green pigment' pyocyanin and the fluorescent yellow-green pigment pyoverdine are produced by:
     Ans: Pseudomonas aeruginosa
047. [pyq_vmc_047] The 'golden yellow' carotenoid pigment staphyloxanthin and coagulase production are major virulence markers of:
     Ans: Staphylococcus aureus
048. [pyq_vmc_048] The causative agent of 'Exudative Epidermitis' (Greasy Pig Disease) in suckling and weaned pigs is:
     Ans: Staphylococcus hyicus
049. [pyq_vmc_049] The Lancefield grouping of Streptococci is based on antigenic differences in their:
     Ans: Cell wall C-carbohydrate (polysaccharide)
050. [pyq_vmc_050] The causative agent of 'Strangles' in horses, characterized by acute purulent pharyngitis and abscessation of mandibular and retropharyngeal lymph nodes, is:
     Ans: Streptococcus equi subsp. equi (Lancefield Group C)
051. [pyq_vmc_051] How many distinct serotypes of Foot and Mouth Disease Virus (FMDV, Aphthovirus) exist worldwide?
     Ans: 7 serotypes (O, A, C, SAT-1, SAT-2, SAT-3, and Asia-1)
052. [pyq_vmc_052] Which serotype of Foot and Mouth Disease Virus (FMDV) is most prevalent and causes the vast majority of outbreaks in India?
     Ans: Serotype O (Ind-2001 / PanAsia lineage)
053. [pyq_vmc_053] Foot and Mouth Disease Virus (FMDV) is rapidly inactivated below which pH threshold?
     Ans: Below pH 6.0 (acid-labile)
054. [pyq_vmc_054] The rabies virus genome consists of which type of nucleic acid?
     Ans: Negative-sense single-stranded unsegmented RNA (-ssRNA)
055. [pyq_vmc_055] Which rabies viral structural protein binds to the nicotinic acetylcholine receptor (nAChR) and NCAM on host neuronal synapses, and is the sole target of neutralizing antibodies?
     Ans: Glycoprotein (G protein)
056. [pyq_vmc_056] The gold standard test recommended by WHO and WOAH (OIE) for post-mortem confirmation of Rabies in brain tissue is:
     Ans: Direct Fluorescent Antibody Test (dFAT)
057. [pyq_vmc_057] The 'Seller’s stain' for demonstration of Negri bodies in rabies impression smears contains:
     Ans: Basic fuchsin and Methylene blue in absolute methyl alcohol
058. [pyq_vmc_058] Peste des Petits Ruminants (PPR) virus belongs to which genus in the family Paramyxoviridae?
     Ans: Morbillivirus
059. [pyq_vmc_059] The live attenuated homologous vaccine strain universally used for control and eradication of PPR in India is:
     Ans: Sungri 96 (PPRV / Sungri / 1996)
060. [pyq_vmc_060] Canine Parvovirus 2 (CPV-2) targets rapidly dividing cells with high mitotic index, primarily destroying:
     Ans: Intestinal crypt enterocytes and bone marrow lymphoid progenitor cells
061. [pyq_vmc_061] The original Canine Parvovirus-2 (CPV-2) emerged in 1978 as a host-range variant of which feline virus?
     Ans: Feline Panleukopenia Virus (FPV)
062. [pyq_vmc_062] The primary structural capsid protein of Canine Parvovirus that determines host range, receptor binding, and antigenicity (including 2a, 2b, 2c variants) is:
     Ans: VP2
063. [pyq_vmc_063] Which virus family is characterized by a reverse transcriptase enzyme converting an RNA genome into proviral DNA that integrates into host chromosomes?
     Ans: Retroviridae
064. [pyq_vmc_064] The 'Coggins test' is the official regulatory agar-gel immunodiffusion (AGID) test for diagnosing:
     Ans: Equine Infectious Anemia (EIA / Swamp Fever)
065. [pyq_vmc_065] Infectious Canine Hepatitis (ICH) in dogs and Rubarth's disease in foxes is caused by:
     Ans: Canine Adenovirus type 1 (CAV-1)
066. [pyq_vmc_066] The Newcastle Disease Virus (NDV) belongs to which family and genus?
     Ans: Paramyxoviridae, Orthoavulavirus (Avian orthoavulavirus 1)
067. [pyq_vmc_067] The virulence of Newcastle Disease Virus strains (velogenic vs lentogenic) is primarily determined by the amino acid sequence at the cleavage site of which surface glycoprotein?
     Ans: Fusion (F) protein
068. [pyq_vmc_068] The 'Intracerebral Pathogenicity Index' (ICPI) in day-old chicks for velogenic Newcastle Disease Virus strains is typically close to:
     Ans: > 1.5 to 2.0 (maximum 2.0)
069. [pyq_vmc_069] Infectious Bursal Disease (IBD / Gumboro disease) virus belongs to which family and possesses which genome structure?
     Ans: Birnaviridae, bisegmented double-stranded RNA (dsRNA)
070. [pyq_vmc_070] The major protective antigen and target of neutralizing antibodies in Infectious Bursal Disease Virus (IBDV) is:
     Ans: VP2 capsid protein (hypervariable loop domain)
071. [pyq_vmc_071] Marek’s Disease in poultry is caused by:
     Ans: Gallid alphaherpesvirus 2 (MDV-1)
072. [pyq_vmc_072] The vaccine strain 'HVT' widely used against Marek's Disease is derived from:
     Ans: Herpesvirus of Turkeys (Meleagrid alphaherpesvirus 1 / MDV-3)
073. [pyq_vmc_073] Classical Swine Fever (Hog Cholera) virus belongs to the genus Pestivirus in which viral family?
     Ans: Flaviviridae
074. [pyq_vmc_074] African Swine Fever Virus (ASFV) is unique among all animal DNA viruses because:
     Ans: It is the only known arbovirus with a double-stranded DNA genome (family Asfarviridae)
075. [pyq_vmc_075] Bluetongue virus in sheep and cattle belongs to the genus Orbivirus (family Reoviridae) and is transmitted biologically by:
     Ans: Culicoides biting midges
076. [pyq_vmc_076] The predominant immunoglobulin isotype in normal adult bovine serum is:
     Ans: IgG1
077. [pyq_vmc_077] In ruminants, the selective transfer of antibodies from maternal circulation into colostrum across mammary alveolar epithelial cells is mediated specifically by:
     Ans: IgG1 via neonatal Fc receptor (FcRn)
078. [pyq_vmc_078] Secretory component (SC) of Secretory IgA (sIgA) is derived from:
     Ans: Polymeric immunoglobulin receptor (pIgR) on mucosal epithelial cells during transcytosis
079. [pyq_vmc_079] Which immunoglobulin isotype exists as a pentamer linked by a J (joining) chain, possesses 10 antigen-binding sites, and is the most efficient at activating the classical complement pathway?
     Ans: IgM
080. [pyq_vmc_080] The 'C1q' subcomponent of the classical complement pathway binds directly to which domain of antigen-bound antibodies?
     Ans: CH2 domain of IgG and CH3 domain of IgM
081. [pyq_vmc_081] The 'Membrane Attack Complex' (MAC) that forms lytic transmembrane channels in target microbial membranes is composed of:
     Ans: C5b, C6, C7, C8, and multiple C9 molecules (C5b-9 complex)
082. [pyq_vmc_082] Major Histocompatibility Complex (MHC) Class I molecules present endogenous peptide antigens to:
     Ans: CD8+ Cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs)
083. [pyq_vmc_083] Type I Hypersensitivity (immediate / anaphylactic) is mediated primarily by:
     Ans: IgE cross-linking on mast cells and basophils triggering degranulation
084. [pyq_vmc_084] The 'Arthus reaction' is a localized cutaneous manifestation of which type of hypersensitivity?
     Ans: Type III Hypersensitivity (Immune Complex-mediated)
085. [pyq_vmc_085] The Tuberculin skin test is a classic clinical example of:
     Ans: Type IV Hypersensitivity (Delayed-Type Cell-Mediated Hypersensitivity / DTH)
086. [pyq_vmc_086] In 'Hybridoma technology' for monoclonal antibody production (Kohler and Milstein, 1975), which selection medium is used to eliminate unfused myeloma cells?
     Ans: HAT medium (Hypoxanthine, Aminopterin, Thymidine)
087. [pyq_vmc_087] Which adjuvant component in Freund's Complete Adjuvant (FCA) triggers intense cell-mediated immunity via NOD2 and Toll-like receptors?
     Ans: Heat-killed dried Mycobacterium tuberculosis / M. butyricum cells in mineral oil
088. [pyq_vmc_088] The 'cigarette ash' or 'chalky powdery' colony on Sabouraud Dextrose Agar with macroconidia shaped like canoes or cigar/pencils is typical of:
     Ans: Trichophyton mentagrophytes
089. [pyq_vmc_089] The dermatophyte of dogs and cats that exhibits bright apple-green fluorescence under Wood's lamp (ultraviolet light at 365 nm) is:
     Ans: Microsporum canis (due to pteridine pigment in hair shafts)
090. [pyq_vmc_090] The 'germ tube test' in fetal bovine serum incubated at 37°C for 2-3 hours is a rapid diagnostic test for:
     Ans: Candida albicans
091. [pyq_vmc_091] The thick mucopolysaccharide capsule of Cryptococcus neoformans is clearly demonstrated microscopically by negative staining with:
     Ans: India ink (Nigrosin)
092. [pyq_vmc_092] Aflatoxins, potent hepatotoxic and carcinogenic mycotoxins commonly contaminating groundnuts and maize, are produced by:
     Ans: Aspergillus flavus and Aspergillus parasiticus
093. [pyq_vmc_093] The 'ring test' or Ring precipitation test of Ascoli for Anthrax is based on which immunological reaction?
     Ans: Precipitation (soluble antigen reacting with hyperimmune serum)
094. [pyq_vmc_094] Which genus of bacteria is characteristically cell wall-less, highly pleomorphic, and completely resistant to beta-lactam antibiotics like penicillin?
     Ans: Mycoplasma
095. [pyq_vmc_095] The primary viral agent of 'Equine Influenza' belongs to which type of Influenza virus?
     Ans: Influenzavirus A (H3N8 and historically H7N7)
096. [pyq_vmc_096] The 'interferon' most potent in activating macrophages and promoting MHC Class II expression is:
     Ans: Interferon-gamma (IFN-gamma, Type II interferon)
097. [pyq_vmc_097] The specific receptor for Foot and Mouth Disease Virus on host epithelial cells is:
     Ans: Integrins (specifically alpha-v beta-6 integrin)
098. [pyq_vmc_098] The 'swarming phenomenon' (concentric waves of spreading growth across non-inhibitory agar media) is characteristic of:
     Ans: Proteus mirabilis and Proteus vulgaris
099. [pyq_vmc_099] Bovine Viral Diarrhea Virus (BVDV) produces which neurological congenital anomaly in calves infected transplacentally between days 100 to 150 of gestation?
     Ans: Cerebellar hypoplasia
100. [pyq_vmc_100] The 'Quellung reaction' (capsular swelling phenomenon observed under brightfield or phase contrast microscopy) is produced by:
     Ans: Antigenic cross-linking of bacterial capsule by homologous anticapsular antibodies increasing its refractive index

========================================
PYQ SUBJECT: VBC (100 questions)
========================================
001. [pyq_vbc_001] The principal rate-limiting and committed enzyme of glycolysis in animal tissues is:
     Ans: Phosphofructokinase-1 (PFK-1)
002. [pyq_vbc_002] In ruminants, what is the single most important volatile fatty acid (VFA) that serves as the major precursor for endogenous gluconeogenesis?
     Ans: Propionate (Propionic acid)
003. [pyq_vbc_003] The enzymatic conversion of Propionyl-CoA to Methylmalonyl-CoA in ruminant liver requires which vitamin as an essential cofactor?
     Ans: Biotin (Vitamin B7)
004. [pyq_vbc_004] The conversion of L-Methylmalonyl-CoA to Succinyl-CoA (which enters the TCA cycle for gluconeogenesis) is catalyzed by Methylmalonyl-CoA mutase, which strictly requires:
     Ans: Vitamin B12 (Deoxyadenosylcobalamin)
005. [pyq_vbc_005] Which enzyme of gluconeogenesis is located exclusively inside the mitochondrial matrix and requires biotin and acetyl-CoA as an obligatory allosteric activator?
     Ans: Pyruvate carboxylase
006. [pyq_vbc_006] Glucose-6-phosphatase, the terminal enzyme of gluconeogenesis and glycogenolysis that releases free glucose into blood, is present in:
     Ans: Liver and renal tubular cortex, but absent in skeletal muscle
007. [pyq_vbc_007] The Pyruvate Dehydrogenase (PDH) multi-enzyme complex requires which five coenzymes for its catalytic cycle?
     Ans: TPP, Lipoic acid, Coenzyme A, FAD, and NAD+
008. [pyq_vbc_008] Which intermediate of the Citric Acid (TCA) cycle is utilized for the synthesis of heme?
     Ans: Succinyl-CoA
009. [pyq_vbc_009] The only substrate-level phosphorylation reaction occurring in the Citric Acid Cycle is catalyzed by:
     Ans: Succinyl-CoA synthetase (Succinate thiokinase)
010. [pyq_vbc_010] Succinate dehydrogenase, the only TCA cycle enzyme embedded in the inner mitochondrial membrane (forming Complex II of the ETC), is competitively inhibited by:
     Ans: Malonate
011. [pyq_vbc_011] The rate-limiting and committed enzyme of the Pentose Phosphate Pathway (Hexose Monophosphate Shunt) is:
     Ans: Glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase (G6PD)
012. [pyq_vbc_012] The essential metabolic role of NADPH generated by the HMP shunt in erythrocytes is to:
     Ans: Maintain Glutathione in its reduced state (GSH) via Glutathione reductase to detoxify H2O2
013. [pyq_vbc_013] Transketolase, an important enzyme in the non-oxidative reversible phase of the HMP shunt, requires which coenzyme?
     Ans: Thiamine pyrophosphate (TPP)
014. [pyq_vbc_014] Domestic animals (cattle, sheep, dogs, cats, horses, pigs) can synthesize ascorbic acid (Vitamin C) from glucose, whereas guinea pigs and primates cannot, because the latter lack which enzyme?
     Ans: L-Gulonolactone oxidase
015. [pyq_vbc_015] Glycogen phosphorylase, the key enzyme of glycogen breakdown, requires which cofactor tightly bound to each subunit?
     Ans: Pyridoxal-5'-phosphate (PLP / Vitamin B6)
016. [pyq_vbc_016] Long-chain fatty acyl-CoA molecules are transported across the inner mitochondrial membrane for beta-oxidation via the:
     Ans: Carnitine shuttle system (CPT-I, Carnitine-acylcarnitine translocase, and CPT-II)
017. [pyq_vbc_017] The primary allosteric inhibitor of Carnitine Palmitoyltransferase-I (CPT-I), preventing simultaneous fatty acid oxidation during de novo lipogenesis, is:
     Ans: Malonyl-CoA
018. [pyq_vbc_018] The complete beta-oxidation of one molecule of Palmitic acid (16:0) yields how many net moles of ATP (assuming P/O ratios of 2.5 for NADH and 1.5 for FADH2)?
     Ans: 106 ATP (108 produced minus 2 used for activation)
019. [pyq_vbc_019] The rate-limiting enzyme of ketogenesis occurring in the mitochondrial matrix of hepatocytes is:
     Ans: HMG-CoA synthase (mitochondrial)
020. [pyq_vbc_020] Which ketone body does NOT react in the Rothera’s nitroprusside test commonly used for detecting clinical ketosis in bovine milk and urine?
     Ans: Beta-hydroxybutyrate
021. [pyq_vbc_021] Why can the liver NOT utilize ketone bodies as an energy source, despite being the sole organ producing them?
     Ans: The liver lacks the enzyme Thiophorase (Beta-ketoacyl-CoA transferase / SCOT)
022. [pyq_vbc_022] Bovine Ketosis (Acetonemia) in high-yielding dairy cows during early lactation is primarily triggered by:
     Ans: Negative energy balance leading to depletion of oxaloacetate for gluconeogenesis and overflow of acetyl-CoA into ketogenesis
023. [pyq_vbc_023] The committed and rate-limiting enzyme of de novo fatty acid synthesis in the cytoplasm is:
     Ans: Acetyl-CoA carboxylase (ACC)
024. [pyq_vbc_024] The multi-enzyme Fatty Acid Synthase (FAS) complex in mammalian and avian tissues carries the growing fatty acyl chain attached to the phosphopantetheine group of:
     Ans: Acyl Carrier Protein (ACP)
025. [pyq_vbc_025] The primary biological donor of reducing equivalents (NADPH) for de novo fatty acid biosynthesis in the cytosol is:
     Ans: Malic enzyme and the HMP shunt (G6PD / 6-PGD)
026. [pyq_vbc_026] The rate-limiting enzyme of cholesterol biosynthesis, targeted pharmacologically by statins, is:
     Ans: HMG-CoA reductase (microsomal / ER)
027. [pyq_vbc_027] Which lipoprotein is responsible for 'Reverse Cholesterol Transport', transporting excess cholesterol from peripheral tissues back to the liver for excretion in bile?
     Ans: High-Density Lipoprotein (HDL)
028. [pyq_vbc_028] The apolipoprotein that acts as an essential cofactor for Lipoprotein Lipase (LPL) on capillary endothelial surfaces, promoting clearance of triglycerides from chylomicrons and VLDL, is:
     Ans: ApoC-II
029. [pyq_vbc_029] Which apolipoprotein is exclusively synthesized in the intestinal enterocytes and is essential for the assembly and secretion of chylomicrons?
     Ans: ApoB-48
030. [pyq_vbc_030] Brown adipose tissue produces non-shivering thermogenesis in newborn lambs and calves through the action of:
     Ans: Thermogenin (Uncoupling Protein-1 / UCP-1)
031. [pyq_vbc_031] All aminotransferases (transaminases) require which coenzyme derived from Vitamin B6?
     Ans: Pyridoxal-5'-phosphate (PLP)
032. [pyq_vbc_032] Which two amino acids do NOT undergo transamination in animal tissues and must be catabolized by direct deamination or other pathways?
     Ans: Lysine and Threonine
033. [pyq_vbc_033] The oxidative deamination of L-glutamate to alpha-ketoglutarate and free ammonia in the mitochondrial matrix is catalyzed by:
     Ans: Glutamate dehydrogenase (GDH)
034. [pyq_vbc_034] The primary rate-limiting and committed enzyme of the Urea Cycle in mammalian hepatocytes is:
     Ans: Carbamoyl Phosphate Synthetase I (CPS-I)
035. [pyq_vbc_035] The obligatory allosteric activator of Carbamoyl Phosphate Synthetase I (CPS-I) in the urea cycle is:
     Ans: N-Acetylglutamate (NAG)
036. [pyq_vbc_036] In the Urea Cycle, the two nitrogen atoms present in the urea molecule are derived directly from:
     Ans: One from free Ammonia (NH4+) and one from Aspartate
037. [pyq_vbc_037] The net energetic cost for the synthesis of one molecule of urea in the urea cycle is:
     Ans: 4 high-energy phosphate bonds (equivalent to 4 ATP hydrolyzed to 2 ADP + 2 Pi and 1 AMP + PPi)
038. [pyq_vbc_038] Why do birds and terrestrial reptiles excrete nitrogen primarily as Uric acid (uricotelism) rather than Urea (ureotelism)?
     Ans: They lack functional carbamoyl phosphate synthetase I and arginase in their liver, conserving water by precipitating semisolid uric acid
039. [pyq_vbc_039] Which amino acid serves as the common precursor for the biosynthesis of the neurotransmitters Dopamine, Norepinephrine, and Epinephrine?
     Ans: L-Tyrosine (derived from Phenylalanine)
040. [pyq_vbc_040] The primary metabolic defect in Classical Phenylketonuria (PKU) is an inherited deficiency of:
     Ans: Phenylalanine hydroxylase (PAH) or its cofactor Tetrahydrobiopterin (BH4)
041. [pyq_vbc_041] The genetic condition 'Alkaptonuria' (characterized by urine turning dark black on exposure to air and alkapton deposition in cartilage) is caused by deficiency of:
     Ans: Homogentisate 1,2-dioxygenase (Homogentisic acid oxidase)
042. [pyq_vbc_042] Maple Syrup Urine Disease (MSUD) in calves and human infants is caused by an enzymatic defect in:
     Ans: Branched-Chain Alpha-Keto Acid Dehydrogenase (BCKDH) complex
043. [pyq_vbc_043] Which amino acid is the direct precursor for the synthesis of the inhibitory neurotransmitter GABA (Gamma-Aminobutyric Acid)?
     Ans: L-Glutamate (via Glutamate decarboxylase requiring PLP)
044. [pyq_vbc_044] S-Adenosylmethionine (SAM), the 'universal methyl donor' in biochemical methylation reactions (e.g. epinephrine synthesis, DNA methylation), is generated from:
     Ans: L-Methionine and ATP
045. [pyq_vbc_045] Creatine, the precursor of high-energy phosphocreatine in skeletal muscle, is synthesized in a multi-organ pathway involving which three amino acids?
     Ans: Glycine, Arginine, and Methionine (as SAM)
046. [pyq_vbc_046] In de novo purine nucleotide biosynthesis, the purine ring is assembled directly on a pre-existing molecule of:
     Ans: 5-Phosphoribosyl-1-pyrophosphate (PRPP)
047. [pyq_vbc_047] The pharmacological agent 'Allopurinol' used in managing hyperuricemia and gout acts as a suicide/mechanism-based inhibitor of which enzyme?
     Ans: Xanthine oxidase
048. [pyq_vbc_048] The enzyme 'Ribonucleotide Reductase' (which reduces ribonucleotides to deoxyribonucleotides for DNA replication) acts at the level of:
     Ans: Ribonucleoside diphosphates (NDPs -> dNDPs)
049. [pyq_vbc_049] Which DNA polymerase in Escherichia coli is primarily responsible for the continuous leading strand and discontinuous lagging strand replication?
     Ans: DNA Polymerase III
050. [pyq_vbc_050] The 5' to 3' exonuclease activity (essential for RNA primer removal and DNA repair during replication) in E. coli is uniquely present in:
     Ans: DNA Polymerase I
051. [pyq_vbc_051] In prokaryotic transcription, the 'sigma factor' of RNA polymerase holoenzyme is required specifically for:
     Ans: Specific promoter recognition and initiation of transcription
052. [pyq_vbc_052] Which eukaryotic RNA polymerase is responsible for the synthesis of messenger RNA (mRNA) and is specifically inhibited by low concentrations of alpha-amanitin (from death cap mushroom)?
     Ans: RNA Polymerase II
053. [pyq_vbc_053] The post-transcriptional '5'-capping' of eukaryotic mRNA consists of which unique chemical modification?
     Ans: 7-Methylguanosine attached via an unusual 5'-to-5' triphosphate bridge
054. [pyq_vbc_054] The 'Wobble Hypothesis' proposed by Francis Crick states that non-Watson-Crick base pairing can occur between the:
     Ans: 3' base of the mRNA codon and the 5' base of the tRNA anticodon
055. [pyq_vbc_055] Which of the following codons functions as both the universal 'Start Codon' for translation and codes for Methionine?
     Ans: AUG
056. [pyq_vbc_056] The three universal 'Stop / Nonsense Codons' that signal the termination of translation in the standard genetic code are:
     Ans: UAA (ochre), UAG (amber), and UGA (opal)
057. [pyq_vbc_057] The peptidyl transferase activity that catalyzes peptide bond formation during ribosomal translation is mediated by:
     Ans: A catalytic ribozyme (23S rRNA in prokaryotes / 28S rRNA in eukaryotes)
058. [pyq_vbc_058] In molecular biology, 'Type II Restriction Endonucleases' are invaluable tools for recombinant DNA cloning because they:
     Ans: Recognize specific symmetrical (palindromic) nucleotide sequences and cleave DNA at precise positions without requiring ATP
059. [pyq_vbc_059] In the Polymerase Chain Reaction (PCR), the optimum temperature typically used for the extension step with Taq DNA polymerase is:
     Ans: 72°C
060. [pyq_vbc_060] The blotting technique used specifically for the detection and sizing of specific RNA sequences using labeled DNA/RNA probes is known as:
     Ans: Northern blotting
061. [pyq_vbc_061] In Michaelis-Menten enzyme kinetics, the Michaelis constant (Km) is defined as:
     Ans: The substrate concentration [S] at which the reaction velocity is half of Vmax
062. [pyq_vbc_062] In a Lineweaver-Burk (double reciprocal) plot of 1/v versus 1/[S], the intercept on the y-axis equals:
     Ans: 1 / Vmax
063. [pyq_vbc_063] A classic 'Competitive Inhibitor' of an enzyme produces which characteristic change in kinetic parameters?
     Ans: Increases Km (decreases apparent affinity) while Vmax remains unchanged
064. [pyq_vbc_064] A 'Non-Competitive Inhibitor' binds to both the free enzyme and enzyme-substrate complex at an allosteric site, producing which kinetic effect?
     Ans: Decreases Vmax while Km remains unchanged
065. [pyq_vbc_065] Which complex of the mitochondrial Electron Transport Chain does NOT pump protons across the inner mitochondrial membrane into the intermembrane space?
     Ans: Complex II (Succinate-CoQ oxidoreductase)
066. [pyq_vbc_066] Complex IV (Cytochrome c oxidase) contains which two essential redox metal prosthetic groups?
     Ans: Heme iron (cytochromes a and a3) and Copper ions (CuA and CuB)
067. [pyq_vbc_067] The deadly poison 'Cyanide' (CN-) and Carbon Monoxide (CO) inhibit cellular respiration by binding specifically to:
     Ans: The ferric (Fe3+) and ferrous (Fe2+) iron of Cytochrome a-a3 in Complex IV
068. [pyq_vbc_068] The antibiotic 'Oligomycin' inhibits mitochondrial oxidative phosphorylation by specifically blocking:
     Ans: The F0 proton channel of ATP Synthase (Complex V)
069. [pyq_vbc_069] Chemical uncouplers of oxidative phosphorylation (such as 2,4-Dinitrophenol / 2,4-DNP) cause:
     Ans: Continued electron transport and oxygen consumption without ATP synthesis, releasing energy as heat
070. [pyq_vbc_070] According to the Chemiosmotic Hypothesis of Peter Mitchell, ATP synthesis by Complex V is directly driven by:
     Ans: The proton-motive force (electrochemical proton gradient across the inner mitochondrial membrane)
071. [pyq_vbc_071] In dogs and cats, which serum enzyme is the most specific indicator of acute hepatocellular injury/necrosis?
     Ans: Alanine Aminotransferase (ALT / SGPT)
072. [pyq_vbc_072] Why is serum ALT NOT a useful diagnostic marker for hepatocellular necrosis in horses, cattle, and sheep?
     Ans: Large animal hepatocytes contain very low basal activity of ALT, rendering it non-diagnostic
073. [pyq_vbc_073] The most sensitive and liver-specific serum enzyme for detecting acute hepatocellular necrosis in horses and cattle is:
     Ans: Sorbitol Dehydrogenase (SDH / Iditol Dehydrogenase)
074. [pyq_vbc_074] Gamma-Glutamyltransferase (GGT) is a membrane-bound brush-border enzyme whose marked elevation in serum is primarily indicative of:
     Ans: Cholestasis, biliary hyperplasia, and bile duct obstruction
075. [pyq_vbc_075] In newborn calves, puppies, and kittens, high serum activity of GGT is clinically utilized as a definitive marker of:
     Ans: Adequate passive transfer of colostral immunoglobulins
076. [pyq_vbc_076] The 'Corticosteroid-induced Isoenzyme of Alkaline Phosphatase' (C-ALP) is unique to which domestic animal species?
     Ans: Dog (Canine)
077. [pyq_vbc_077] The most sensitive, specific, and gold-standard biomarker for evaluating acute skeletal muscle necrosis (e.g. exertional rhabdomyolysis / tying-up in horses, capture myopathy, white muscle disease) is:
     Ans: Creatine Kinase (CK)
078. [pyq_vbc_078] Cardiac Troponin I (cTnI) is the diagnostic biomarker of choice in veterinary medicine for detecting:
     Ans: Myocardial injury, necrosis, or active myocarditis
079. [pyq_vbc_079] The serum creatinine concentration is widely used to assess the Glomerular Filtration Rate (GFR) because:
     Ans: It is produced at a constant rate from muscle creatine and phosphocreatine, freely filtered by the glomerulus, and neither significantly reabsorbed nor secreted
080. [pyq_vbc_080] The 'Anion Gap' in clinical blood gas analysis is calculated using which formula?
     Ans: [Na+ + K+] - [Cl- + HCO3-]
081. [pyq_vbc_081] The visual pigment Rhodopsin present in retinal rod cells consists of the apoprotein Opsin covalently bound to:
     Ans: 11-cis-retinal
082. [pyq_vbc_082] The active hormonal form of Vitamin D synthesized in the proximal convoluted tubules of the kidney by 1-alpha-hydroxylase is:
     Ans: 1,25-Dihydroxycholecalciferol [Calcitriol / 1,25-(OH)2-D3]
083. [pyq_vbc_083] Vitamin E (alpha-tocopherol) and Selenium act synergistically in biological membranes because:
     Ans: Vitamin E breaks peroxyl free radical chain reactions in lipid bilayers while Selenium is an essential component of cytosolic Glutathione Peroxidase
084. [pyq_vbc_084] Sweet clover poisoning (dicoumarol toxicosis) and warfarin rodenticide poisoning in animals produce severe coagulopathies by inhibiting:
     Ans: Vitamin K epoxide reductase (VKOR)
085. [pyq_vbc_085] 'Polioencephalomalacia' (PEM / Cerebrocortical necrosis) in ruminants is clinically caused by a functional deficiency of:
     Ans: Thiamine (Vitamin B1) or excess dietary sulfur
086. [pyq_vbc_086] 'Curled-toe paralysis' in growing broiler chicks is pathognomonic for dietary deficiency of:
     Ans: Riboflavin (Vitamin B2)
087. [pyq_vbc_087] 'Black tongue' in dogs and pellagra in humans and pigs are classic deficiency syndromes of:
     Ans: Niacin (Nicotinic acid / Vitamin B3) or its precursor Tryptophan
088. [pyq_vbc_088] 'Grass Tetany' (Hypomagnesemic tetany) in lactating cattle grazing lush green spring pastures is precipitated primarily by:
     Ans: Low blood magnesium levels caused by high dietary Potassium (K+) and Nitrogen interfering with ruminal magnesium absorption
089. [pyq_vbc_089] 'Enzootic Ataxia' and 'Swayback' in newborn and young lambs characterized by demyelination of the spinal cord and cerebral white matter is caused by deficiency of:
     Ans: Copper (Cu)
090. [pyq_vbc_090] 'Parakeratosis' in swine, characterized by non-inflammatory hyperkeratinized crusty skin lesions on limbs and snout, is caused by deficiency of which trace mineral?
     Ans: Zinc (Zn)
091. [pyq_vbc_091] The enzyme responsible for the conversion of Glucose to Sorbitol in the polyol pathway (implicated in diabetic cataractogenesis) is:
     Ans: Aldose Reductase (utilizing NADPH)
092. [pyq_vbc_092] In iron transport and metabolism, which plasma beta-1 globulin is responsible for transporting ferric iron (Fe3+) in systemic circulation?
     Ans: Transferrin
093. [pyq_vbc_093] The primary storage form of iron in hepatocytes, splenic macrophages, and bone marrow is:
     Ans: Ferritin
094. [pyq_vbc_094] The rate-limiting and committed enzyme of porphyrin and heme biosynthesis in hepatocytes is:
     Ans: Delta-Aminolevulinic Acid Synthase (ALA Synthase / ALAS-1)
095. [pyq_vbc_095] Which amino acid is the primary precursor for the synthesis of Thyroid hormones (T3 and T4) within thyroglobulin in thyroid follicles?
     Ans: L-Tyrosine
096. [pyq_vbc_096] The intracellular secondary messenger cyclic AMP (cAMP) is degraded and inactivated into 5'-AMP by which enzyme?
     Ans: Phosphodiesterase (PDE)
097. [pyq_vbc_097] Which trace element is an essential catalytic component of the enzyme Glutathione Peroxidase (GSH-Px)?
     Ans: Selenium (Se, as Selenocysteine)
098. [pyq_vbc_098] The remethylation of Homocysteine to Methionine in animal tissues requires which two vitamins acting cooperatively?
     Ans: Vitamin B12 (Methylcobalamin) and Folic Acid (N5-methyl-THF)
099. [pyq_vbc_099] In collagen biosynthesis, the post-translational hydroxylation of Proline and Lysine residues strictly requires:
     Ans: Ascorbic Acid (Vitamin C), Ferrous iron (Fe2+), and Alpha-ketoglutarate
100. [pyq_vbc_100] In small animal veterinary practice, marked elevations of serum Amylase and Lipase along with elevated Spec cPL (canine pancreatic lipase immunoreactivity) are diagnostic for:
     Ans: Acute Pancreatitis
